Cheesy Stuffed Shells

Serves 6
Ingredients
  • olive oil
  • kosher salt
  • 340 g jumbo pasta shells
  • 160 g prosciutto sliced
  • 30 g garlic minced and separated, ½ for spinach, ½ for marinara
  • 225 g spinach
  • 100 g yellow onion grated
  • 25 g chopped Calabrian chiles chopped, substitute chili flakes
  • 75 g tomato paste
  • Water a splash to deglaze the pot
  • 800 g crushed tomatoes canned
  • 7 g kosher salt
  • 20 g sugar
  • 10 g basil chiffonade
  • 450 g ricotta cheese
  • 100 g heavy cream
  • 75 g shredded mozzarella
  • 75 g grated parmesan plus extra for topping
  • 1 large egg
  • 20 cranks of fresh black pepper
  • olive oil for cooking
  • 2000 g whole milk
  • 200 g heavy cream
  • 15 g salt
  • 100 g white distilled vinegar
Instructions
  1. Pour milk and heavy cream into a saucepan. Add 15g of salt. Heat gently over medium-low heat, stirring frequently, until it reaches 190°F/88℃ (about 5-8 minutes).
  2. Remove from heat and swirl in vinegar. Carefully and gently stir it in then let sit for 15 minutes until curds form.
  3. Line a strainer with cheesecloth and gently scoop the curds into the cloth. Let the cheese drain for 20-30 minutes.
  4. Bring a pot of salted water to a boil. Add the jumbo shells and cook for about 6 minutes, until almost al dente. Drain and toss gently with olive oil to prevent sticking. Set aside.
  5. Stack prosciutto, slice it into strips, then finely mince into small pieces.
  6. Heat a Dutch oven over medium heat with a drizzle of olive oil. Add the minced prosciutto and fry for 90 seconds, being careful not to crisp it.
  7. Add the first half of the garlic and cook for another minute. Stir in spinach and cook until wilted, about 2 minutes.
  8. Transfer to a cutting board, chop finely, and squeeze out any excess moisture with a towel.
  9. Wipe out the Dutch oven. Add a drizzle of olive oil, then grated onion and remaining garlic. Add a pinch of salt and sweat the mixture for 5 minutes. Stir in chopped Calabrian chiles (or chili flakes) and fry for 2-3 minutes.
  10. Add tomato paste and cook for 1 minute until it deepens in color. Pour in the can of crushed tomatoes, 7g salt, sugar, and a splash of water. Simmer for 5 minutes, then stir in chiffonade basil. Taste and adjust seasoning.
  11. In a medium bowl, combine the drained homemade ricotta, the prosciutto-spinach mixture, heavy cream, shredded mozzarella, 75g of grated parmesan, about 20 cranks of black pepper, and 1 large egg. Stir until well combined.
  12. Preheat the oven to 425°F/220C.
  13. Fill each shell with about 2-3 tablespoons of the ricotta mixture, tucking the cheese inside using your fingers or a spoon.
  14. Nestle the stuffed shells in the sauce (I just do this in my dutch oven, but you can transfer to a baking dish if needed), arranging 18-22 shells in a crown pattern, making sure they are at least halfway submerged.
  15. Sprinkle a generous amount of grated parmesan over the shells. Cover the dish with a lid and bake for 15 minutes.
  16. Remove the lid and bake uncovered for another 15 minutes to allow the cheese to brown and the sauce to thicken slightly.
  17. Top with additional grated parmesan.Scoop out 3-4 shells per serving, top with more grated parmesan, and drizzle with a bit of spicy finishing olive oil.

Chicago Deep Dish Pizza (New & Improved Recipe)

Serves 1
Ingredients
  • 375 g AP flour
  • 10 g salt
  • 40 g unsalted butter cold, cubed
  • 220 g warm water 90F/32C
  • 6 g instant yeast
  • 50 g unsalted butter
  • 100 g white onion grated, 1 small or ½ large
  • 20 g garlic minced
  • 800 g crushed tomatoes canned
  • 800 g whole peeled tomatoes canned
  • 75 g tomato paste
  • 10 g salt
  • 20 g sugar
  • 2 g dried oregano
  • 2 g dried basil
  • 2 g crushed red pepper/chili flakes
  • 450 g 80/20 ground pork
  • 7 g salt
  • 10 g sugar
  • 5 g paprika
  • 3 g dried sage
  • 2 g black pepper
  • 2 g red chili flake
  • 5 g garlic grated or finely minced
  • 3 g fennel seed toasted, crushed
  • 10 g red wine vinegar
  • deep dish pizza pan i’m using a 12″x2.25″ Lloyd pan linked here, but a 12″ cast iron pan will also work well
  • olive oil
  • dough recipe above
  • 12-15 slices full fat mozzerella sliced into a medium thickness
  • 5-6 slices of provolone sliced very thinly
  • 225 g hot italian sausage recipe above
  • pepperoni thinly sliced
  • grated parmesan cheese
Instructions
  1. Dissolve the yeast into the warm water.
  2. Add flour, salt, and cold butter to a food processor and spin for about 15 seconds or until butter is mixed into the flour then stream water/yeast mixture into the flour/butter in the food processor while it’s spinning.
  3. Spin for 45-60 seconds until you have a mass that has come together into a mass that’s somewhere between dough and a pie crust.
  4. Transfer the dough into a bowl, then round it into a ball shape, tucking the ball up under itself as you go to create some tension across the surface of the ball.
  5. Cover the dough with a lid or plastic wrap and allow to ferment at room temperature for about 30 minutes.
  6. Refrigerate, covered, for 12-24 hours.
  7. Heat a sauce pan over medium high heat. Add in butter and allow to mostly melt, then add onion and garlic stir to combine and cook for 4-5 minutes, stirring occasionally, until fragrant and soft.
  8. While the onions cook, in a large bowl or container, combine the tomato products and use your hands mix and squish the whole tomatoes into smaller ½”/1.5cm pieces. You could also blitz a few times with an immersion blender if you prefer, but the hand method gives you more control and creates a nice rustic texture.
  9. Into the tomato mixture, stir in salt, sugar, oregano, basil, and chili flakes.
  10. Back to the onions/garlic on the stove… When the onions and garlic have softened and have taken on some color, add the tomato mixture into the sauce pan. Lower heat to medium and reduce the sauce by 40-50% which should take 15-20 minutes, stirring occasionally. When done, the sauce should be quite thick and hold a bit of a trail when you pass a spoon through it.
  11. Combine all ingredients and mix well until combined and slightly tacky.
  12. Remove dough from the refrigerator and allow to come to room temperature for about 30-45 minutes before rolling it out. When it’s ready to roll it should have increased in size by about 30%. If it hasn’t, proof a bit longer at room temperature before rolling out.
  13. Preheat oven to 425F/220C while you make your sauce and prep the rest of your ingredients.
  14. Add a very generous amount of olive oil to your pizza pan, spreading it around to coat the bottom and sides of the pan.
  15. Flour your work surface and flip dough out onto the work surface, flouring both sides of the dough. Pat a bit to degas, then roll it out into a 14″/35cm round, adding a bit more flour to any areas that are sticking to the rolling pin along the way.
  16. Flip the rolled out dough over one hand/arm then gently lay it into the oiled pizza pan, pressing the dough up the sides of the pan and into the corners, ensuring there aren't any air bubbles underneath.
  17. Shingle on 12-15 slices of the medium-thick full fat mozzarella, followed by 5-6 slices of provolone.
  18. Next add about half of the italian sausage (8oz) in quarter-sized pieces.
  19. Spoon on half of the sauce then spread into an even layer to cover.
  20. Top that with 25-30 slices of thin pepperoni.
  21. Finally, finish that with a generous amount of grated parmesan.
  22. Bake at 425F/220C for 45 minutes.
  23. After baking, remove pizza from the pan and allow to cool on a wire rack for about 10 minutes to keep the crust crisp.
  24. Return to the pizza pan to cut (this keeps it warmer a little longer) or cut it on a cutting board for cleaner slices.
  25. Serve and garnish with more parmesan.

Chicken Al Pastor Tacos

Serves 4
Ingredients
  • 50 g pineapple diced
  • 100 g orange juice 2 fruits worth
  • 25 g water
  • 25 g white vinegar
  • 20 g sugar
  • 20 g kosher salt
  • 30 g achiote annatto paste or sub in lime juice, chile powder, & paprika
  • 2 g dried oregano
  • 5 g garlic powder
  • 5 g cumin ground
  • 10 g chile powder
  • 1 kg boneless skinless chicken thighs
  • 4 “planks” of fresh pineapple cut in similar size/thickness to chicken
  • 75-100 g white onion small diced
  • 100 g poblano small diced, 1 whole
  • 350-400 g tomatillos medium chopped, 5-6 whole
  • 15-20 g garlic minced, 5 cloves
  • 5 g kosher salt
  • 50 g water
  • corn tortillas heated
  • oil
  • chopped cilantro
  • white onion small diced
  • queso fresco or feta
Instructions
  1. Puree all ingredients until smooth.
  2. Add ½ of marinade or 100g to chicken and mix to coat.
  3. Lay thighs onto a foil lined baking sheet close together in the center of the tray. Place 2 planks of pineapple along each side of the chicken.
  4. Preheat broiler on high. Place chicken/pineapple on rack closest to broiler and let cook for 5minutes. make sure to keep a close eye to be sure it doesn't burn.
  5. Rotate tray if needed, cook for another 5 minutes. Chicken should be nicely charred on top at this point.
  6. Flip chicken and return to broiler for another 5-8 minutes to char 2nd side.
  7. Add onion, poblano, tomatillos, garlic, salt, water to a saucepan over med-high and bring to a simmer.
  8. Once simmering, lower heat to medium low, cover and cook for about 10min.
  9. Stir and cook for another 8-10 minutes over medium, uncovered, to allow to thicken.
  10. Transfer to cold bowl and cool in fridge or freezer.
  11. Chop chicken and pineapple into 1/2″/1.5cm pieces.
  12. Add long squeeze of oil to a nonstick pan over high/med-high, add in chicken/pineapple. Fry on first side for about a minute.
  13. Add 40-50g reserved marinade to pan. Toss to combine and saute until sauce is reduced.
  14. Place chicken on double layer corn tortilla, add chopped cilantro and white onion, tomatillo salsa, sprinkle of grated queso fresco.

Chicken And Dumplings

Serves 8
Ingredients
  • 1 kg boneless skinless chicken thighs
  • kosher salt for seasoning
  • black pepper for seasoning
  • 300 g white onion medium diced
  • 150 g celery medium diced, 3-4 stalks
  • 150 g carrots diced, 3-5 medium
  • 150 g fennel chopped, 1 medium bulb
  • 20 g garlic minced/pressed, 4-5 cloves
  • 115 g unsalted butter 1 stick
  • 135 g all-purpose flour sub with cup for cup gluten free flour
  • 100 g dry white wine
  • 1200 g chicken broth
  • 100 g heavy cream
  • 3-4 sprigs thyme
  • 2 bay leaves
  • 2 g poultry seasoning
  • 200 g frozen peas
  • 360 g all-purpose flour
  • 8 g kosher salt
  • 12 g baking powder
  • 4 g black pepper
  • 75 g unsalted butter melted
  • 340 g whole milk
Instructions
  1. Preheat the oven to 400°F (205°C).
  2. Season both sides of the chicken thighs with salt and black pepper. Roast in the oven for 30 minutes or until fully cooked and fat is rendered.
  3. While the chicken is roasting, dice onion, celery, carrots, fennel. Press garlic cloves.
  4. In a large heavy-bottomed pot over medium heat, melt one stick of butter. Add diced vegetables, garlic, and a pinch of salt. Sweat the vegetables for 6-8 minutes, stirring often. I'm using a 6 ¾ qt dutch oven in this recipe.
  5. Stir in the all-purpose flour to create a roux. Cook for 1 minute and deglaze with wine, scraping any fond off the bottom of the pot. Cook for about 30 seconds to cook off the alcohol.
  6. Add in the chicken broth, heavy cream, thyme, bay leaves, and poultry seasoning. Stir well.
  7. Chop the roasted chicken thighs into small bite sized pieces and add to the pot over medium heat along with frozen peas. Stir and simmer on low for 5 minutes to reheat chicken and peas, scraping the bottom of the pot as you go.
  8. In a separate bowl, mix the dry dumpling ingredients: all-purpose flour, salt, baking powder, black pepper. While mixing, stream in the melted (but not hot) butter, and milk and continue stirring until just barely combined.
  9. Taste the chicken gravy mixture for seasoning and adjust with additional salt if needed.
  10. Back to the dumpling dough, using two teaspoons, quenelle the dumpling dough and drop 12-16 dumplings into the pot.
  11. Lower the heat to low, cover with a lid, and cook for 15 minutes or until the dumplings have expanded and cooked through.
  12. Test a dumpling for doneness by cutting into it; it should be fluffy and cooked through, not gluey.
  13. Once the dumplings are cooked, the chicken and dumplings are ready to be enjoyed.

Chicken Noodle Soup

Serves 8
Ingredients
  • 1000 g chicken backs
  • 750 g chicken feet
  • 2700 g chicken stock or water
  • 2700 g chicken stock or water
  • 30 g Better Than Bouillon chicken
  • 21 g packets gelatin 3 packets, 7g each
  • 200 g leeks (white and light green parts) sub yellow onion, medium dice
  • 400 g carrots medium dice
  • 300 g celery medium dice
  • 30 g chicken fat or oil
  • 550 g boneless, skinless chicken thighs
  • 10 g kosher salt plus a little more for additional seasoning
  • black pepper ground, for seasoning
  • 300 g egg noodles
  • kosher salt
Instructions
  1. Preheat oven to 400℉.
  2. Roast the chicken backs and feet in the oven for 20 minutes or until chicken is just starting to brown.
  3. Deglaze the sheet tray with a bit of stock to get up all of the leftover fond. Combine chicken, stock and fond into a pressure cooker and cook on high pressure for 45 minutes to an hour. Allow the pressure to come down naturally.
  4. Skim the stock and get rid of 80% of the fat on top.
  5. In a gallon size container, whisk together ingredients and set aside.
  6. Trim excess fat from the chicken. Salt each side of the chicken thighs and set aside.
  7. Bring a pot of water to a boil.
  8. While the pot of water is coming up, grab a dutch oven and set it on the stove. Heat up a few tablespoons of oil and sweat down leeks, carrots and celery on medium high heat.
  9. After 4-5 minutes, or when the leeks are becoming translucent, add in stock and bring to a boil.
  10. When stock is up to a boil, lower to a simmer and add in the chicken. Simmer for 15 minutes, or until the chicken is at an internal temp of 180 or higher and can be easily shredded.
  11. While the chicken is cooking, cook noodles to desired doneness, about 12 minutes. Strain the noodles, toss them in oil and lay out on a sheet tray and place in the refrigerator.
  12. Remove the chicken from the soup and shred. After the chicken has been shredded, run a knife through it once or twice to get it into smaller pieces. Return chicken to the soup and add 10g of salt. Taste the soup and adjust seasoning as needed with more salt and pepper.
  13. To serve, pour soup in a bowl over the cooked noodles and top with a few cranks of black pepper.
  14. Refrigerate any leftover noodles for up to 3 days. Any leftover soup will be good for 5-7 days in the refrigerator and up to 3 months in the freezer.

Chicken Parm

Serves 2
Ingredients
  • 340 g boneless skinless chicken thighs 2 thighs, 6 oz each
  • kosher salt
  • 125 g all-purpose flour
  • 2 large eggs beaten
  • 75 g panko breadcrumbs
  • 75 g parmesan cheese grated
  • 2 g black pepper
  • 2 g kosher salt for breading mixture
  • 200 g olive oil for frying
  • 2 slices provolone cheese
  • 6 slices fresh mozzarella
  • 30 g grated parmesan cheese for topping
  • olive oil
  • 30 g minced garlic about 6 cloves
  • 25 g chopped Calabrian chiles sub with 2–3g dried chili flakes
  • 75 g tomato paste
  • 800 g crushed tomatoes canned
  • 20 g sugar
  • 10 g salt
  • 10 g fresh basil chiffonade
Instructions
  1. Season both sides of the chicken thighs generously with salt. Let rest for 10 minutes.
  2. Place thighs in a freezer bag, one at a time, and pound it gently with a saucepan until it is about 7–8 inches long, 3 inches wide, and ½ inch thick. (20cmx7.5cmx1.25cm)
  3. Prepare three trays: one with flour, one with beaten eggs, and one with a breadcrumb mixture (panko, parmesan, pepper and salt – mixed).
  4. Coat each thigh thoroughly in flour, shaking off excess. Dip in beaten eggs to coat, ensuring no dry spots remain. Finally, press into the breadcrumb mixture, packing the breading tightly so no chicken is showing. Repeat for both thighs.
  5. Place the breaded cutlets on a wire rack and refrigerate for 30 minutes to ensure the breading adheres during frying.
  6. Heat a sauce pan over medium heat and add olive oil (~15g).
  7. Add minced garlic and chopped Calabrian chiles. Cook until garlic is soft and fragrant (~3–4 minutes).
  8. Stir in tomato paste and cook for 60 seconds until it deepens in color.
  9. Add crushed tomatoes (blended lightly with an immersion blender for a smoother texture), sugar, and salt. Simmer on low heat for 15 minutes until thickened.
  10. Chiffonade basil. Turn 90 degrees and chop again. Stir in off heat.
  11. Heat 200g/1c of olive oil in a large sauté pan to 350–375°F (177–190°C). Lay the breaded chicken carefully in the hot oil and fry for ~2 minutes on the first side until golden brown. Flip and fry for another 90–120 seconds.
  12. Transfer fried cutlets to a wire rack.
  13. Spread ¼ cup marinara sauce over each fried chicken cutlet.
  14. Place 1 slice of provolone cheese and 3 slices of fresh mozzarella on each cutlet.
  15. Sprinkle with grated parmesan cheese.
  16. Place cutlets under a broiler set to high for 3–4 minutes until the cheese is melted, bubbling, and browned. (If no broiler, bake at the highest oven setting for 6–8 minutes.)

Chicken Tinga

Serves 8
Ingredients
  • 1200 g chicken thighs
  • kosher salt
  • cooking oil
  • 100 g white onion rough chopped
  • 15 g garlic sliced
  • 25 g tomato paste 1
  • 400 g fire roasted tomatoes
  • 200 g fresh tomato rough chopped
  • 200 g chicken stock
  • 10 g Better Than Bouillon chicken flavor
  • 2 chipotle chilies in adobo sauce
  • 1 g dried oregano
  • 20 g apple cider vinegar
  • 25 g cilantro stems about a handful
  • 5-8 g kosher salt
  • tostada shells
  • little gem or romaine lettuce shredded
  • crema sour cream whisked with some lime juice
  • cilantro leaves
Instructions
  1. Preheat the oven to 400℉.
  2. Lay out the chicken thighs on a sheet tray and generously salt each. Roast in the oven for about 20 minutes until they reach about 180°F/82°C.
  3. While chicken is cooking (and cooling), make the sauce. Add oil to a sauce pan over medium heat, then add onions, garlic, and a pinch of salt, and sweat over medium heat until translucent, about 5-6 minutes.
  4. Add tomato paste to the pan stir and cook for about 2 minutes until it begins to take on color, and then add in the fire roasted tomatoes. Stir and bring to a simmer.
  5. Next pour in chicken stock, Better Than Bouillon, chipotles, and oregano. Stir and cook for about 15 minutes on medium low until slightly thickened and the fresh tomatoes are fully cooked.
  6. Stir in vinegar, 5-8g salt, and cilantro stems then using an immersion blender, blend together the sauce until smooth. Return to the stove and keep warm.
  7. When the chicken is cool enough to touch, shred it and add it to the sauce. Stir and allow chicken to heat through. Taste for seasoning and adjust if needed.
  8. To build a tostada, briefly heat the tostada in the oven and top with tinga, shredded lettuce, crema and cilantro.

Chili Crisp Peanut Noodles

Serves 0
Ingredients
  • 225 g wheat noodles ½lb
  • 200 g broccolini, chopped 1-2 bunches
  • 30 g oil, divided 2T
  • 450 g ground beef 1lb
  • 125 g chili crisp 2/3c
  • 50 g soy sauce 3T
  • 50 g mirin 3T
  • 50 g water 3T
  • 60 g unsweetened peanut butter ¼c
  • 40 g hoisin 2T
  • Optional: chopped peanuts scallions for garnish
Instructions
  1. Cook noodles per package. Drain + rinse w/ cold water. Toss w/ oil.
  2. Stir-fry broccolini in 1T oil + pinch salt 90–120 sec over high. Transfer to bowl.
  3. Brown beef in 1T oil over high, 5–6 min until crumbled and brown.
  4. Add chili crisp. Stir and scrape 1 min.
  5. Add soy, mirin, water, peanut butter, hoisin. Stir, bring to simmer.
  6. Add noodles & broccolini. Toss well.

Chili Oil Wonton Recipe

Serves 34
Ingredients
  • 30-40 square wonton wrappers
  • 450 g ground pork
  • 4 g white pepper
  • 10 g sesame oil
  • 10 g soy sauce
  • 10 g garlic (2 cloves) minced
  • 10 g ginger minced
  • 25 g scallions sliced
  • 10 g salt
  • 5 g granulated sugar
  • 1 egg large
  • 400 g peanut oil
  • 20 g gochugaru divided into 2 equal portions
  • 10 g red pepper flakes
  • 15 g sesame seeds
  • 2 g Szechuan peppercorns ground
  • 20 g garlic (4-5 cloves) minced
  • 50 g honey
  • 2 g MSG
  • 15 g Chinese black vinegar
  • 30 g soy sauce
  • 60 g hot wonton poaching water
Instructions
  1. In a heat-resistant bowl, combine aromatics: 10g (half) the gochugaru, red pepper flake, sesame seeds, ground Szechuan peppercorn, and minced garlic. Set aside.
  2. In a small saucepan, combine the remaining 10g of gochugaru with the peanut oil. Place over medium heat and bring to 350°F/175°C. When the mixture starts to sizzle, the chili powder turns a darker red, and the oil becomes a deep orangish-red color, it’s ready to strain. This usually takes 4-5 minutes.
  3. Carefully strain the hot oil over the bowl of aromatics. You’ll hear a sizzle and smell the chili and garlic bloom. Stir gently to coat evenly.
  4. Once the oil mixture has cooled for about 10 minutes to the point that you can easily touch the side of the bowl, add the honey, soy sauce, black vinegar, and MSG. Stir to combine. Set it aside while you make the filling.
  5. In a large bowl, combine the filling ingredients: ground pork, white pepper, sesame oil, soy sauce, garlic, ginger, scallions, salt, sugar, and egg.
  6. Mix by hand and aggressively agitate the meat for 4-5 minutes or until the texture turns from being loose into a paste-like sticky mass that holds itself together a bit.
  7. To wrap the wontons, place a wonton wrapper on a work surface (keep wrappers-in-waiting under a damp towel to stay moist). Add 15g (1 tbsp) of filling to the center. Wet the edges of the wrapper, fold one corner over to the opposite corner to form a triangle, pressing out any remaining air as you seal. Then crimp the edges of the wrapper around the filling 5-6 times, gathering them up into a beggar’s purse shape. Squeeze the wrapper tightly to make sure it's sealed and has no air pockets. Place each finished wonton on a sheet tray lined with parchment paper.
  8. Cover the sheet tray with a lid or towel and refrigerate for at least 30 minutes.
  9. Bring a pot of well-salted water to a high simmer, just under rolling boil. Use a spider strainer to add the wontons slowly in batches of 12-15, allowing them to set up for 10 seconds in the water before releasing. Reduce heat to low-ish (around 190°F/90°C) and cook at just below a simmer for 4 minutes per batch.
  10. When cooked, pull the wontons and dab off any excess water before transferring to serving bowl.
  11. Before serving, whisk 60g (about ¼ cup) of the starchy poaching water into the chili oil. The starch emulsifies the oil into a glossy, sauce-like glaze. Generously spoon the chili oil over the wontons.
Full recipe →

Chopped Cheese Sandwich

Serves 0
Ingredients
  • 225 g or 8oz 90/10 Ground Beef per sandwich
  • 1 c cold fine shredded iceberg lettuce per sandwich
  • Thinly sliced tomatoes
  • Thinly sliced white onions
  • Garlic mayo 55g/¼c mayo combined with 2 minced garlic cloves
  • Steak seasoning mix of salt, pepper, paprika, dried garlic, dried onion, red pepper flakes
  • Pepperoncini rings
  • 2 slices american singles per sandwich
  • 2 slices provolone per sandwich
  • Ketchup
  • Hoagie roll**
  • High smokepoint oil canola, light olive, grapeseed, etc
Instructions
  1. Prep all condiments and toppings above before cooking the meat.
  2. Work the ground beef back and forth in your hands to form the beef into a large flat patty.
  3. Slice hoagie in half, leaving one side still attached. Remove some of the soft bread from the center of the hoagie. Heat for a a few minutes in a 400F/205C oven to crisp just before building sandwich.
  4. Smear a generous spoonful of garlic mayo on both sides of hoagie.
  5. Add a long glug/squeeze of high smokepoint oil to preheated cast iron skillet (over high) followed by beef patty. Use a sturdy spatula to press the beef down into the pan for optimal contact. Season top side with a pinch of steak seasoning and top with shaved onions and allow meat to brown for about 45-60 seconds.
  6. When bottom side of beef is golden brown and crusty, flip beef onto the onion side. Use your spatula or a metal scraper to chop the beef until broken down. Season with another pinch of steak seasoning and continue to cook for at least 60-90 seconds until beef has taken on some nice color and onions are browned and cooked.
  7. Pile up cooked meat into a rough “hoagie” shape then layer on cheese slices. Cook for about 20-30 seconds or long enough for cheese to melt.
  8. Lift the melty mass of beef and cheese onto the hoagie roll.
  9. Top with lettuce, a few pepperoncinis, tomato slices, a tiny sprinkle of steak seasoning, and a nice long zig zag of ketchup. Wrap sandwich in foil or parchment, slice in half and prepare for sandwich euphoria.

Chow Mein

Serves 2
Ingredients
  • 300 g Hong Kong pan-fried noodles cut into 5–7 in lengths
  • 150 g chicken thigh thinly sliced
  • 75 g scallions cut into 1½ in pieces, greens only
  • 75 g carrots julienned
  • 75 g bean sprouts
  • high smoke point oil
  • kosher salt to season
  • 30 g soy sauce
  • 30 g oyster sauce
  • 5 g sesame oil
  • 100 g chicken stock
Instructions
  1. Heat a wok over medium-high. Add in oil and then chicken. Season lightly with salt. Sear the chicken undisturbed for about 60 seconds, then toss and cook through another 60-90 seconds. Remove the chicken from the wok and set aside.
  2. Add more oil into the wok. Add in the noodles and extra oil on top. Toss and detangle; fry until lightly stiff and fragrant.
  3. Stir in the carrots and scallions. Stir-fry for 2–3 minutes until just softened. Feel free to add in more oil if it is a little dry.
  4. Add in the bean sprouts and toss briefly. Return the chicken to the wok and toss to combine.
  5. Pour sauce around hot edge. Toss and fold over high heat until sauce steams and lightly softens noodles without making them mushy.

Classic American Deli-Style Egg Salad

Serves 8
Ingredients
  • Yield: 8 portions
  • 24 large eggs
  • 6 L water
  • Ice
  • Warm water
  • Paper towels
  • 250 g mayonnaise
  • 45 g Dijon mustard
  • 15 g minced chives
  • 15 g chopped dill
  • 25 g white distilled vinegar
  • 15 g sugar
  • 10 g salt
  • 5 g black pepper
  • 5 g paprika
  • 20 g finely chopped celery 2 stalks
  • Nice sourdough bread
  • Durkees famous sauce
  • Green leaf lettuce
Instructions
  1. Fill a very large pot with 6-7 quarts of water and bring it to a boil.
  2. Carefully lower eggs into the boiling water using a spider or long spoon to prevent cracking.
  3. Set a timer for 13 minutes for large eggs (adjust time for medium or jumbo eggs).
  4. After 13 minutes, remove the pot from heat and add ice (as much as will fit into the pot) to stop the cooking process.
  5. Drain some warm water from the pot, add more ice, and run cold water over the eggs for 2-3 minutes or until the warm water is circulated out of the pot
  6. Peel the cooled eggs under warm water, cracking them in several places to make peeling easier. Pat the peeled eggs dry to remove excess moisture.
  7. Set aside 8 of the eggs. Reserving their yolks for later.
  8. Press the remaining 16 eggs through a wire rack to “dice” them evenly.
  9. In a separate bowl, combine mayo, Dijon, minced chives, chopped dill, vinegar, sugar, salt, pepper, paprika, and chopped celery.
  10. Smash 6 of the reserved egg yolks into the mayo mixture and stir until well combined.
  11. Gently fold the mayo mixture into the diced hard boiled eggs until evenly coated.
  12. Crumble the last 2 reserved egg yolks into the mixture to absorb excess moisture and provide texture.
  13. To make each sandwich, spread Durkees famous sauce onto two slices of sourdough bread. Add washed green leaf lettuce on one slice of bread and spoon 3-4 large spoonfuls of egg salad on top.

Classic Chicago Italian Beef

Serves 0
Ingredients
  • 150 g or about 1 1/3c each of Cauli Celery, Green Bell Peppers, Carrots
  • 75 g or 1/2 c serrano or another hot pepper of choice
  • 400 g or 1 3/4c white distilled vinegar
  • 200 g or 3/4c water
  • 24 g or 1 1/2 tbsp salt
  • 300 g or 1 1/3c light olive oil
  • 150 g or 3/4c ex virgin olive oil
  • 225 g or 1c water 86F/30C
  • 9 g or 3tsp yeast
  • 20 g or 1 1/2tbsp olive oil
  • 15 g or 1 1/4 tbsp sugar
  • 350 g or 2.5c bread flour 12.7% protein
  • 9 g 1 1/2 tsp salt
  • Two 2lb .9kg top round roasts, cut in half (1lb for each sandwich roll)
  • Neutral cooking oil canola, vegetable, etc
  • 1000 g or 1L nice beef stock recipe below
  • Spoonful bouillon paste optional
  • Salt to taste
  • Bman’s Super Secret Beef Spice Blend: 5g each dried oregano garlic powder, onion powder, toasted coriander seed, chili flake, paprika, toasted fennel seed, black pepper.
  • 6-8 lbs or 3-4kg beef shanks
  • 2000 g or 2L water
Instructions
  1. Chop all veg into small dice and mix to combine.
  2. Combine vin, water, salt in a sauce pan, bring to a boil, add veg, stir, turn off heat and allow to cook as it comes to room temp.
  3. Transfer to container and refrigerate 2 days.
  4. After 2 days, strain liquid off veg.
  5. To the strained veg mix, add oils & store in fridge until ready to use.
  6. Combine ingredients into bowl of stand mixer with dough hook. Mix on low until combined (3min).
  7. When dough has begun to form into a ball, increase to high speed and continue mixing for another 6 mins.
  8. Transfer to a medium bowl, cover, and allow to ferment at room temp for 90 mins.
  9. Flour dough and work surface and divide into two 300g pieces.
  10. Preshape into balls as shown in video @2:40. Cover with towel and allow to rest for 15 min.
  11. Shape each piece as shown @3:00. Place onto parchment lined sheet tray dusted with semolina. Place shaped dough rolls onto tray and gently press down to slightly flatten.
  12. Cover and let proof for 30-45min until they’ve grown by about 75%.
  13. Score lengthwise.
  14. Bake in a 375F/190C preheated oven for about 30 min (rotating halfway through) until light golden grown.
  15. Preheat large pot on med-high, add a few plugs of neutral cooking oil, and add in 2 pieces of the roast. Sear well on each side.
  16. Add beef stock to deglaze.
  17. Add spice blend. Bring to simmer, cover with parchment and load into 250F/120C oven to cook for 60 min. When done, meat temp should be 140F/61C.
  18. Wrap beefs in plastic wrap and allow to cool in fridge.
  19. Strain cooking liquid into container and store in fridge.
  20. When ready to eat a beef, slice meat as thinly as possible.
  21. For the Jus, add spoonful bouillon paste to the reserved cooking liquid, heat to 145F/62C, and season to taste (should be very salty) and lower heat to low.
  22. Add in sliced beef to just warm through.
  23. Roast shanks in very hot oven (450F/230C) for 30min.
  24. Add to slow cooker along with all fond from roasting pan and water. Slow cook for 6hrs.

Classic Southern Style Baked Mac & Cheese

Serves 0
Ingredients
  • 225 g or 1/2lb Sharp cheddar shredded
  • 225 g or 1/2lb Colby jack shredded
  • 225 g or 1/2lb American cheese shredded (sub velveeta)
  • 125 g or 1c grated parmesan
  • 450 g or 1lb macaroni
  • 3 eggs
  • 125 g or 1/2c sour cream
  • 2 12 oz or 350ml cans evaporated milk
  • 2 g or 1tsp black pepper
  • 8 g or 1 3/4tsp salt
  • 115 g or 1 stick unsalted butter melted
Instructions
  1. Stir macaroni into well salted boiling water and cook per package instructions or until al dente (should still have a strong bite and not be totally soft).
  2. Drain off water and add the noodles into a large bowl.
  3. Mix all cheeses together into a separate bowl.
  4. In another separate bowl make egg custard by whisking together 3 large eggs, sour cream, evaporated milk, salt and pepper.
  5. Add egg custard, melted butter, and about 85-90% of the shredded cheese blend into the large bowl with the cooked macaroni. Gently stir together. Mixture will be very runny.
  6. Pour cheesy noodle mixture into a 9”x13” (23x33cm) baking dish, smooth down the top, and top with reserved shredded cheeses. Bake at 375F/190C for 35-40 min until cheese on top is golden and melted.

Coq Au Vin

Serves 4
Ingredients
  • 1500 g skin on chicken drumsticks about 10
  • 750 mL dry red wine
  • 150 g thick cut bacon about 4 pieces
  • 225 g pearl onions sub sliced shallot
  • 225 g carrots large diced
  • 1 lb crimini mushrooms quartered
  • olive oil
  • kosher salt
  • 25 g tomato paste
  • 25 g minced garlic 4-5 cloves
  • 75 g cognac
  • 40 g ap flour
  • 300 g full bodied chicken stock or 300g water + 25g Better than Bouillon + 3 gelatin packets
  • 2 springs thyme
  • 2 bay leaves
  • 200 g dried egg noodles
  • 50 g pasta water reserved from cooking pasta
  • 100 g cold butter
Instructions
  1. Add drumsticks into ziplock bag and add 750mL of wine. Marinate in the fridge for 20-30 minutes.
  2. Remove chicken from wine and lay drumsticks out on a parchment lined sheet tray. Save the wine for later. Dry chicken on all sides well. Bake at 450°F/230°C for 20-30 minutes.
  3. To prep the rest of the ingredients, slice the bacon into lardons (rectangles). Cut off the pearl onion poles and peel. Cut carrots into large dice, clean and quarter the mushrooms
  4. Preheat dutch oven over medium heat. Add about 2 tbsp of olive oil followed by bacon. Stir and render bacon for 10-12 minutes, stirring often, until browned. Transfer to a paper towel lined plate, leaving as much bacon fat in the dutch oven as possible.
  5. Return pot to heat and add mushrooms and a pinch of salt. Stir and add about 2 tbsp of water if needed to release bacon fond. Stir and continue to cook for 5-6 min until they've softened and have begun taking on color.
  6. Add in pearl onions, carrots, and a large pinch of salt. Stir and sweat until veggies have lost some moisture. Stir in tomato paste and garlic. When tomato paste has become a rusty color, deglaze with cognac, scraping up fond in the pot as you go.
  7. When cognac has evaporated, add flour. Stir and cook until pasty. Add reserved wine from chicken marinade. Add full bodied chicken stock. Once stirred in, add rendered bacon and bring to a simmer.
  8. Add chicken back into the dutch oven with broth and veggies along with any resting juices, making sure to submerge the meat as much as possible.
  9. Add herbs and top with a parchment lid.
  10. Load dutch oven (with parchment lid) into a 350°F/175°C oven to braise for 40-50 minutes.
  11. Remove the pot from the oven to check the chicken texture. It should be pull apart, but still a bit firm and not totally fall apart. Once it's at that point, remove chicken from the pot and set aside.
  12. Heat cooking liquid and veggies over medium high and bring to a simmer to reduce for 10-15 minutes. Season to taste with salt.
  13. Finally, add chicken back into the reduced sauce once more and baste to reheat. Serve over egg noodles.
  14. Boil to package instructions, reserving about ½ cup of pasta water. Drain, return pasta to pot with starchy water and cold butter. Stir and toss to emulsify butter with water and glaze noodles.

Corned Beef and Cabbage

Serves 0
Ingredients
  • 1000 g water mL
  • 250 g cups coarse salt 1 – 1 1/4
  • 100 g granulated sugar 1/2c
  • 15 g pink salt 2 1/2t
  • 4-5 bay leaves
  • 15 g mustard seeds 5t
  • 15 g coriander seed 2T
  • 15 g 2Tblack peppercorns 2T
  • 5-6 cloves garlic smashed
  • 1000 g ice 2.5qt
  • 5-6 lb brisket point 2.5kg
  • Water as needed to cover the beef
  • Garlic sachet smashed garlic, bay leaves, coriander seeds, black peppercorns, and mustard seeds tied up in a cheesecloth
  • 1 giant carrot 200-250g/2c, cut into large obliques
  • 1 small head of green cabbage peeled and cut into 10 pieces
  • 12 small red potatoes halved
  • Horseradish Cream Sauce:
  • 125 g sour cream 1/2c
  • 75 g mayo 1/3c
  • 3 g coarse salt 2/3t
  • 15 g water 1T
  • Black pepper large pinch of 12 cranks of grinder
  • 75 g prepared horseradish 5T
Instructions
  1. In a saucepan, combine water, salt, sugar, pink salt, bay leaves, mustard seeds, coriander, black peppercorns, and smashed garlic. Bring to a hard boil for 5 minutes.
  2. Pour the hot brine over 1000 grams of ice to cool it down quickly. Submerge the brisket into the cooled brine and refrigerate for 5-7 days.
  3. Rinse the brined beef to remove excess saltiness and pat it dry.
  4. Preheat a large heavy bottomed pot over medium-high. Add a generous amount of high smokepoint oil (2Tbsp or so) and sear beef for 4-5 minutes on each side.
  5. Add enough water to cover 90% of the beef, and place the garlic sachet in the pot.
  6. Bring to a boil, cover, and transfer to a 275°F135C oven to braise for 5-7 hours or until tender. The meat fibers should have some nice give, but the beef shouldn’t be complete falling apart.
  7. Transfer the corned beef into a baking dish along with a couple of ladles full of the cooking liquid. Cover with foil and rest in 200F/95C oven to stay warm while you cook the vegetables.
  8. Put the beef cooking liquid back on the stove over med-high heat to bring to a simmer.
  9. Cut carrot into large obliques.
  10. Peel cabbage and cut in half. Cut each half into about 5 wedges, cutting through the core on each piece to hold each wedge together.
  11. Halve the small red potatoes.
  12. Add cut cabbage, potatoes, and carrots to the simmering cooking liquid and gently poach over low heat (200F/95C) for about 15 minutes or until potatoes are cooked through.
  13. Combine sour cream, mayo, salt, water, pepper, and horseradish.

Creamy Ground Beef Stroganoff

Serves 0
Ingredients
  • 2 lb/1kg 85/15 ground beef
  • Salt
  • Pepper
  • 125 g 1 whole white onion, small diced
  • 20 g 4-5 cloves garlic, minced/pressed
  • 1 lb/450g baby bella mushrooms sliced
  • Oil
  • 25 g 1 1/2T tomato paste
  • 50 g 6T ap flour
  • 175 g 3/4c dry white wine
  • 40 g 2 1/2T worcestershire
  • 500 g 2c water
  • 21 g 3 packets powdered gelatine
  • 50 g 3T Better than Bouillon beef
  • 12 oz/340g egg noodles
  • 225 g 1c heavy cream
  • 75 g 1/3c sour cream
  • 30 g 2T dijon mustard
  • 5 g 2t black pepper
  • Dill
  • Chives
Instructions
  1. Spread beef into a thin layer on a rimmed baking sheet. Season with salt and pepper. Roast on high under broiler (2nd from top rack) for 10-15 min, rotating halfway through. Allow to cool for 5-10 min before crumbling meat.
  2. To cook, heat a dutch oven or heavy bottomed pot over medium high. Add about 3T oil and the sliced mushrooms and a pinch of salt. Stir and saute for 2-3 min until water has released and mushrooms are browned and softened.
  3. Stir in onions, garlic, and a pinch of salt, scraping bottom of pot to deglaze and scrape up mushroom fond. Stir in tomato paste and flour and cook for 1-2 mins until tomato paste has turned from red to rusty in color.
  4. Stir in wine stirring and scraping fond from bottom of pot. Reduce until wine evaporates.
  5. Whisk together water, gelatine, and better than bouillon. Add into pot with mushrooms and veg and bring to a simmer.
  6. Add in crumbled beef with any drippings. Cook and reduce for about 10 minutes to thicken.
  7. While that thickens, cook noodles in salty water to just past al dente. Drain and shock with cold water to stop cooking. Return noodles to their cooking pot, add 1-2T olive oil and stir.
  8. Stir heavy cream into the stroganoff and cook and reduce for about 10 more minutes, stirring often.
  9. Stir in sour cream, dijon, and black pepper.
  10. To finish, combine cooked egg noodles with stroganoff. Garnish with fresh chives and dill.

Creamy Mushroom White Sauce Pasta

Serves 2
Ingredients
  • 30-40 g dried mushrooms shitake or porcini
  • 500 g water
  • 375 g fresh mushrooms sliced, i’m using king trumpet, enoki, and oyster
  • olive oil
  • 30 g unsalted butter
  • kosher salt
  • 50 g shallot minced
  • 20 g garlic minced
  • 2-3 g fresh thyme
  • 225 g campanelle pasta
  • 250 g heavy cream
  • 50-60 g aged parmesan grated
  • black pepper
  • lemon juice
Instructions
  1. For the mushroom broth, add dried mushrooms and 500g water to sauce pan, bring to a boil, reduce heat to med-low and simmer for 5-10 minutes. Remove from heat and allow to seep for 10-15 more minutes.
  2. Preheat a large saute pan over med-high and add a squeeze of olive oil and butter. When butter has melted, add fresh sliced mushrooms and a generous pinch of salt (about 5-6g). Stir to combine and cook for 5-6 minutes, stirring occasionally.
  3. At this point, the fresh mushrooms should be about halfway cooked. Add shallots, garlic, thyme, and pinch of salt. Stir and cook for another 3-4 minutes. Add in about ½ of the dried mushroom broth (liquid only) to deglaze. Cook and reduce until fully evaporated and shallots are tender, about 3min. Add cream, bring to a simmer, and reduce by about half.
  4. Bring a large pot of water to a boil over high and salt heavily. Add pasta to water and cook to al dente. Drain off pasta and add it to the mushroom cream sauce pan.
  5. If the mixture is dry, add additional mushroom broth, about 100-150g. Toss and stir to combine.
  6. Off heat, add grated parmesan, black pepper, and a small squeeze of lemon juice. Toss to combine. Adjust consistency with more parmesan if it's too thin or with mushroom broth if it's too thick.
  7. Taste and add salt if needed. Garnish by topping with a sprinkle of parmesan, black pepper, and minced chives.

Crispy Korean Fried Chicken

Serves 4
Ingredients
  • 1250 g bone in, skin on chicken thighs
  • 20 g kosher salt
  • 10 g ginger grated/minced
  • 10 g garlic grated/minced
  • light olive oil
  • 350 g ap flour plus more to coat the chicken
  • 350 g potato starch
  • 10 g baking powder
  • 8 g onion powder
  • 8 g garlic powder
  • 15 g kosher salt
  • 60 g soy sauce
  • 40 g sugar
  • 110 g honey
  • 100 g gochujang
  • 60 g mirin
  • 30 g hot sauce
  • 20 g ginger grated/minced
  • 20 g garlic grated/minced, 4-5 cloves
  • 200 g water
Instructions
  1. Fill a large, heavy bottomed pot with 3" or so/7cm of oil. For my 6 ¾ qt pot, i use about 3L. More importantly, oil should be no more than 3" or 7cm from the top of the pot. Preheat over medium heat. You're looking for the oil to reach a frying temp of 350°F/176°C.
  2. Cut thighs into 4 pieces, depending on the size of the thigh (3 pieces if thighs are smaller). Toss to evenly coat with 20g salt, ginger, and garlic. Refrigerate for 15-20 minutes.
  3. Place cut chicken thighs to a towel and try well on all sides.
  4. Whisk together ap flour, potato starch, 15g salt, baking powder, garlic powder and onion powder in a large bowl.
  5. Into a second bowl add about ⅓ of the dry flour mixture and just enough water until the consistency is somewhere between loose pancake batter and cream. Add a drizzle or about a cup of the wet mixture back into the dry flour mixture. Stir and fluff until it’s crumbly and and very little powdery dry flour remains.
  6. Add the dried chicken into an empty bowl and toss with enough plain flour to coat each piece well. Toss between your hands to remove excess flour and set chicken on a tray to begin battering/dredging process.
  7. One by one, drop each piece of chicken into the wet batter to evenly coat. Shake/drain off any excess and move into the crumbly/craggy “dry” mixture to coat. Place on a tray.
  8. Carefully drop thighs into preheated oil, making sure that chicken pieces aren't touching – do this in 2 batches if needed. Fry for 7 minutes. While frying, check temp and adjust if needed to keep oil at 350°F/176°C. Remove chicken from oil and drain on wire rack.
  9. Increase oil heat to 375°F/190°C and lower once-cooked chicken back into oil to cook for another 5min until golden brown.
  10. Brush gochujang sauce onto as many of the pieces as you’d like.
  11. Combine ingredients in a small sauce pot and bring to a simmer over high/med-high for about 15 minutes. Sauce should have reduced by 4-5 times.
  12. Brush onto fried chicken and garnish with sesame seeds.

Crockpot Italian Beef

Serves 0
Ingredients
  • 4 lbs 2kg beef chuck
  • Kosher salt
  • Olive oil
  • 1 32 oz box 900g beef stock
  • 10 g onion powder
  • 10 g garlic powder
  • 5 g dried oregano
  • 5 g dried basil
  • 3 g chili flakes
  • 5 g coriander
  • 5 g paprika
  • 50 g Worcestershire sauce
  • 25 g tomato paste
  • 50 g beef bouillon I use Better Than Bouillon
  • 150 g ¼ head cauliflower florets
  • 150 g 4 stalks celery, diced
  • 150 g 2 medium carrots, diced
  • 150 g 1 whole green bell pepper, diced
  • 150 g 1 small white onion, diced
  • 75 g 4-5 serrano chilies, thinly sliced
  • 5-6 cloves garlic pressed
  • 600 g white distilled vinegar
  • 400 g water
  • 30 g salt
  • 300 g canola oil
  • 200 g olive oil
  • 225 g warm water
  • 9 g instant yeast
  • 20 g olive oil
  • 15 g sugar
  • 350 g bread flour
  • 9 g salt
Instructions
  1. Cut beef chuck into 5-6 large chunks, about 2-3 inches across.
  2. Season beef generously with kosher salt (approximately 2-3 tbsp or 30g) and let it sit for 15 minutes.
  3. Heat olive oil in a large non-stick pan over medium-high heat. Sear the beef chunks for 5-10 minutes until browned on two sides.
  4. Add beef to a slow cooker. I like to use my instant pot for this.
  5. Deglaze the pan with a splash of water or stock, then pour it over the beef in the slow cooker.
  6. Add stock, onion powder, garlic powder, oregano, basil, chili flakes, coriander, paprika, worcestershire, tomato paste, and beef bouillon to the slow cooker. Stir to combine. Slow cook on low for 8-10 hours or until tender.
  7. Remove the beef from the slow cooker and shred it into smaller pieces, but not fully shredded.
  8. Heat olive oil in a pan over medium-high heat and refry the shredded beef for 4-5 minutes until it’s a littlel crispy around the edges.
  9. Add 2-3 cups of beef jus to the pan and reduce by half.
  10. To assemble the sandwiches, slightly crisp up the Italian rolls in the oven for 5 minutes.
  11. Layer giardiniera, shredded refried Italian beef, and concentrated jus on the rolls.
  12. Wrap the sandwiches tightly with parchment paper.
  13. GIARDINIERA
  14. Break down cauliflower into small florets. Small dice celery, carrots, bell pepper, onion. Thinly slice chiles and press or mince garlic. Combine in a bowl.
  15. In a pot, bring vinegar, water, and salt to a boil. Add in the vegetables, remove from heat, cover, and let them cool in the brine. Once cool, marinate for at least 8 hours in the refrigerator.
  16. The next day, drain the pickling liquid and top the vegetables with canola oil and olive oil.
  17. Preheat the oven to 375°F (190°C).
  18. Combine Ingredients in a stand mixer bowl fitted with a dough hook.
  19. Mix on medium speed for 3 minutes or until dough is well combined and clearing the bowl, then increase to high speed and mix for 4 more minutes until the dough is fully developed and you can tug on it without it ripping.
  20. Transfer dough to a bowl and round it into a ball. Cover and ferment at room temperature for 90 minutes. It should have doubled in size.
  21. Divide the dough into two pieces, shape them into balls, cover, and let them rest for 15 minutes.
  22. Shape each piece of dough into a 12-inch tube (see video @7:00) and place them onto the back of a sheet tray lined with parchment paper.
  23. Cover and proof for 60 minutes. They should double in size.
  24. Spray the rolls with water, score them tip to tip with a razor blade or serrated knife, and bake for 30 minutes at 375°F (190°C) until golden brown.

Crunchy Tacos

Serves 0
Ingredients
  • 3 T olive oil 45ml
  • 2 lb 90/10 ground beef 1kg
  • Salt
  • 2 small white onions small diced
  • 25 g minced garlic 5 cloves
  • 40 g chili powder 1/3c
  • 10 g paprika 3 1/2t
  • 10 g ground cumin 5t
  • 2 g Chile flakes 1 1/4t
  • 2 g dried oregano 1t
  • 500 g chicken stock 2c
  • 200 g tomato sauce 3/4c
  • 20 g brown sugar 5t
  • 30 g apple cider vinegar 2T
  • 400 g tomato sauce 1 1/2c
  • 25 g guajillo chiles 4 dried
  • 300 g water 1 1/4c
  • 20 g sugar 5t
  • 10 g paprika 3 1/2t
  • 10 g garlic powder 2t
  • 3 g cumin 1t
  • 2 g oregano 1t
  • 10 g onion powder 2 3/4t
  • 10 g salt 2t
  • 100 g apple cider vinegar 1/3c
  • 2 heads romaine lettuce
  • 8 oz pepper jack cheese 225g
  • 8 oz sharp cheddar cheese 225g
  • 12-16 corn tortillas
  • Neutral oil for frying
  • Fresh tomatoes for garnish
  • Sour cream
Instructions
  1. In a heavy-bottomed pot, heat olive oil over medium heat. Add ground beef and a pinch of salt. Thoroughly crumble the beef as it browns.
  2. Once the beef is browned and crumbled, add onions, garlic, and a pinch of salt. Cook until the onions become translucent.
  3. Stir in chili powder, paprika, ground cumin, chili flakes, and dried oregano. Cook for 20-30 seconds until fragrant.
  4. Add chicken stock, tomato sauce, brown sugar, apple cider vinegar, and a pinch of salt. Bring to a simmer and cook for 15 minutes until thickened. Taste and adjust salt if necessary.
  5. Meanwhile, prepare the taco sauce (BRI-TEGA): soften dried guajillo chiles removing stems and seeds, boil water, turn off heat, and place chilis in the water to soften for about 15 min.
  6. In a blender, combine tomato sauce, softened guajillo chiles, water, sugar, paprika, garlic powder, cumin, oregano, onion powder, salt, and apple cider vinegar. Blend until very smooth.
  7. Preheat the oven to 185°F (85°C). Keep a sheet tray inside to keep taco shells warm.
  8. Shred the romaine lettuce finely and grate the pepper jack and sharp cheddar cheese.
  9. To fry the taco shells, fill a 4-quart saucepan about ⅔-¾ full with neutral oil and heat to 325°F (163°C). Use taco frying tongs to fry each corn tortilla for about 90 seconds until lightly golden brown. Transfer to the oven to keep warm.
  10. 9a. Alternatively, in a 10″ saute pan, pour enough oil to reach about 1 inch depth and preheat to 325°F (163°C). Fry one half of the corn tortilla for about 25-30 seconds until starting to firm up. Then flip and submerge the other half, holding one side at an angle with tongs, and pressing the submerged side into the oil until golden brown. Flip and refry the first side for an additional 30-60 seconds until crispy.
  11. Once the taco shells are ready, assemble the tacos by filling each shell with a quarter cup of the taco meat mixture, a pinch of the cheese blend, a generous drizzle of BRI-TEGA sauce, shredded lettuce, and diced fresh tomatoes. Add sour cream and raw onions if you’d like.

Curried Chicken Salad

Serves 2
Ingredients
  • 450 g chicken breast about 2-3 breast, 16oz
  • kosher salt
  • 50 g shallot minced
  • 50 g jalapeños small diced
  • 10 g garlic minced or microplaned, 2 cloves
  • 25-30 g lime juice 1 lime
  • 15 g curry powder
  • 3 g cumin
  • 5 g garam masala
  • 30 g minced cilantro
  • 30 g pistachios
  • 50 g pickled golden raisins + 2 spoonfuls brine recipe below
  • 8 g kosher salt
  • 200 g mayo homemade is best – recipe below – but I use Duke's when I don't have the time
  • 250 g white distilled vinegar
  • 125 g sugar
  • 150 g golden raisins
  • 1 large egg
  • 20 g water
  • 20 g white distilled vinegar
  • 5 g kosher salt
  • 325 g neutral oil avocado, canola, grapeseed, etc
Instructions
  1. To cook the chicken, salt well on both sides then allow to sit and dry brine for around 15-20 minutes.
  2. Boil a pot of water, then reduce the heat to low until water temp reaches 180°F/82°C (to test water temp, I just use my meat thermometer)
  3. Stir a very large handful of salt into the water then gently drop in the chicken breasts. This should drop the water temp to about 165°F/75°C. Poach for 20 minutes at a temp of 165°F/75°C.
  4. When cooked, the internal temp of the chicken should read 150-155°F/65-68°C.
  5. To prep the pistachios, chop them then sift off the nut dust using a fine mesh strainer.
  6. Add all ingredients including sifted pistachios into a stand mixer with paddle attachment and mix on high for about 30 seconds to mix ingredients and shred down the chicken a bit.
  7. If you’re not using a stand mixer, just cut down chicken well, then mix vigorously to shred it up a bit.
  8. Serve on a toasted brioche bun, crackers, or fresh naan.
  9. Heat sugar & vinegar in saucepan over med until simmering.
  10. Add raisins in and stir.
  11. Remove from the heat and allow to plump for 20 minutes.
  12. Add all ingredients except oil into a high sided container. Blend with an immersion blender then slowly stream in oil. When you get to the last of the oil, it may start pooling on top of the mayo. If that happens, move the immersion blender to the top of the mayo and work it up and down a bit to incorporate the oil into the mixture.

Dad Ribs

Serves 4
Ingredients
  • 2 full slabs St. Louis style ribs
  • 30 g salt coarse kosher
  • 15 g black pepper
  • 15 g garlic powder
  • neutral oil for grilling
  • neutral oil for sauteeing
  • 100 g onion grated (100g = ½ an onion)
  • 15 g garlic (15g = 4 cloves)
  • 250 g ketchup
  • 100 g molasses
  • 30 g Worcestershire sauce
  • 20 g hot sauce I prefer Frank's
  • 20 g apple cider vinegar
  • 20 g yellow mustard
  • 5 g chili powder
  • 1 g cayenne
Instructions
  1. Remove the membrane by sliding a butter knife between the membrane and back of a rib bone at one end of the slab. Twist the knife to create space then slide a finger underneath to loosen. Grab the flap with a dry paper towel and rip it off.
  2. Approximate the halfway point and cut both slabs in half between the bones.
  3. Stir together salt, pepper, and garlic powder. Divide into fourths and pat a portion into each half slab to thoroughly cover.
  4. Refrigerate on a wire rack, uncovered for 12-18 hours to dry brine.
  5. Preheat the oven to 275°F/135°C. Wrap the ribs in two layers of foil, set the packet on a sheet pan, and roast for 3 hours.
  6. After 3 hours of roasting, pull at the meat with 2 forks to check for doneness. At this point it should be shreddable, but only if you give it a little effort.
  7. Open the foil and let the ribs sit for 20 minutes so the juices can resettle into the meat before we hit them on the grill.
  8. Grate onion on a box grater until pulpy, then run knife thru to fully break down. Then press the garlic.
  9. Heat a drizzle of oil in a small saucepan over medium and cook the onion and garlic for 5 minutes until softened and translucent.
  10. Once aromatics are softened. Add ketchup, molasses, Worcestershire, hot sauce, apple cider vinegar, mustard, chili powder, and cayenne. Bring to a simmer, drop heat to low, and cook with a lid half covering pan for 10 minutes or until it's reduced by about 25%. Taste and set it aside.
  11. Preheat grill to high and spray liberally with pan spray or wipe with oil. Spray ribs with pan spray and drop them, meat-side down, onto grate and grill 1-2 minutes. Flip and sauce liberally with BBQ sauce on the meaty side. Close lid and cook for 1-2 minutes.
  12. If your grill gets super hot, deduce heat to medium-high at this point. Then carefully flip a second time and sauce the bone side. Grill meat side down again for 1-2 minutes, lid closed, or until there is some nice char.
  13. Flip ribs a final time and sauce meat side again. Close lid and grill for 1-3 more minutes or until the sauce on top has thickened into a sticky lacquer.
  14. Remove ribs from grill, slice between, the bones and serve immediately.

Detroit Style Pan Pizza

Serves 2
Ingredients
  • 250 g warm water 90F/32C
  • 3 g instant yeast
  • 9 g salt
  • 365 g bread flour
  • olive oil
  • 800 g 800g crushed tomatoes canned
  • 50 g tomato paste
  • 7 g salt
  • 12 g sugar
  • 1/2 g dried oregano
  • 1/2 g dried basil
  • 1 pinch chili flakes
  • Olive oil
  • 10 g garlic minced
  • 250 g Wisconsin brick cheese shredded, sub with full fat mozzarella
Instructions
  1. Mix together water, yeast salt, and bread flour in a bowl with a spoon. When the mix forms into a ball and you can no longer mix with a spoon, wet your hand and continue mixing and kneading by hand for 1-2 more minutes.
  2. Alternatively, you can use a stand mixer with dough hook and mix on med-high for 7-8 minutes. Cover the dough with a lid and allow to ferment at room temperature for 30 min.
  3. Perform 5-6 strength building folds then round and tuck the dough into a ball to create tension. Cover and ferment at room temperature. Repeat the strength building folds and rounding and tucking. Now, cover and allow to ferment at room temperature for 1 hour. Finally, cover and ferment in the refrigerator for 8-24 hours.
  4. Flip dough onto a floured surface and flatten into a rough square shape. Cut dough into 2 equal halves. Oil 2 lloyd detroit pizza pans very well with olive oil. Add a dough half into each pan and use your fingertips to dimple the dough and stretch it into the pans. Cover and allow to proof for about 30 minutes in a warm place, 90F/32C (to achieve this, i heat an oven to it's lowest setting for 2 minutes, then turn it off).
  5. Dock the doughs with your fingertips once more to fully spread into the corners of the pans. Cover again and allow to proof at room temp for 30 more minutes.
  6. Add tomato paste, salt, sugar, dried oregano, basil, and chilli flakes into the 28oz can of crushed tomatoes and blend with an immersion blender until smooth.
  7. Add a large drizzle of olive oil to a nonstick pan over medium heat, then add in minced garlic and saute until just fragrant, about 30-60 sec. Stir in tomato mixture and bring to a simmer and cook down for about 10 minutes or until reduced by half and thickened.
  8. Preheat oven (with pizza steel or stone) to 500F/260C. After proofing the dough for the final time, top each pizza with about 125g/1/4lb of the shredded brick cheese, then add 4 generous spoonfuls of sauce and spread slightly, leaving some of the top of the pizza unsauced. Bake in the pans on top of the pizza steel for 10-14 minus until cheese is melted and browned around the edges. Remove pizzas from their pans and allow to cool on a wire rack for 5 minutes. To increase crispiness, load pizzas directly onto the pizza steel for an additional 3-5 minutes.

Detroit Style Pizza

Serves 2
Ingredients
  • 240 g water 80-85 degree farienheit
  • 2 g rapid rise yeast
  • 335 g bread flour
  • 7 g kosher salt
  • Two 8″x10″ Lloyd pans or 8″x8″ or 9″x9′ baking pan, but crust may be less crisp
  • 50 g oil to coat pans olive, avocado, etc
  • 800 g crushed tomato
  • 50 g tomato paste
  • 7 g kosher salt
  • 10 g sugar
  • 1/2 g dried basil
  • 1/2 g dried oregano
  • 1 g chili flake
  • 2 garlic cloves minced
  • 2 TBSP olive or avocado oil
  • 300 g brick cheese grated on the largest holes of grater, if you can’t find brick cheese, I recommend a blend of Monterey Jack and mozzarella
  • 1 stick of pepperoni sliced about 1/16″ thick
  • grated parm
  • any other toppings of your choice
Instructions
  1. Combine ingredients into a medium bowl and stir with sturdy spoon
  2. Once it comes together, use a wet hand to give the dough a series of 20-30 slap and folds as shown in the video. You can also use a stand mixer with a dough hook if preferred.
  3. Cover bowl with plastic and let it sit in warm spot for 30 minutes.
  4. After 30 min, build more strength into the dough by grabbing an of dough, stretching it out, and folding back over. Rotate the dough 45-90 degreed and repeat 5-6 more times see video for technique.
  5. After these stretch and folds, tuck and fold the dough a few more times as shown in the video to shape and stretch the dough into a taught ball.
  6. Wrap bowl with plastic and set in warm place for 2 hours. After the 2 hours dough should have doubled in size, be buoyant, and slightly bounce back when poked.
  7. To prep pans for dough, glug in 2 tbsp oil into each pan and smear over bottom and sides
  8. Flip dough onto floured surface and divide in half.
  9. Lay half of the dough into each pan and gently press to spread.
  10. Cover pans and allow dough to relax for 30 minutes.
  11. After the 30 minutes, gently stretch and press dough to cover the bottom of the pans
  12. Cover again and let rise for 45-60mins.
  13. Pour 75% of the can of tomatoes into a tall sided container
  14. To that, add tom paste, salt, sugar, basil oregano, and chile flake
  15. Blend with and immersion blender, or use a standard blender
  16. Add the remaining 25% of crushed tomatoes and stir
  17. Preheat medium saute pan on medium
  18. Add oil and garlic
  19. when garlic is golden and fragrant, add tomato mixture and stir to combine.
  20. Simmer for about 25 minutes or until thickened
  21. Preheat oven and pizza stone to 500 f
  22. Spread shredded brick cheese over the dough
  23. Spoon sauce in two thick lines, lengthwise down each dough, leaving the cheese uncovered in the middle and on either side of the lines of sauce, see video
  24. Add pepperoni, or toppings of choice, on top followed by grated parm
  25. Load pan directly on top of pizza stone and bake for 16-18 minutes. I recommend baking each pizza separately, one at a time, for ultimate crispiness.

East Coast Bar Pizza

Serves 4
Ingredients
  • 300 g water
  • 6 g instant yeast
  • 40 g olive oil
  • 525 g all purpose flour
  • 10 g kosher salt
  • 800 g crushed tomatoes canned
  • 100 g tomato paste
  • 12 g kosher salt
  • 20 g sugar
  • 2 g garlic powder
  • 2 g onion powder
  • 2 g black pepper
  • 2 g dried basil
  • 2 g dried oregano
  • 1 g crushed red pepper/red Chile flakes
  • 450 g full fat aged mozzarella shredded
  • 225 g young white cheddar shredded
  • grated parmesan
  • flakey salt I use Maldon
  • dough recipe above
  • sauce recipe above
  • 450 g 80/20 ground pork
  • 10 g kosher salt
  • 8 g sugar
  • 2 g black pepper
  • 2 g garlic powder
  • 1 g oregano
  • 1 g dried sage
  • 5 g paprika
  • 1 g chile flake
  • 5 g red wine vinegar
  • 2 g crushed fennel
Instructions
  1. Add ingredients to stand mixer with dough hook and mix on medium for 2-3 minutes or until a loose ball forms. Increase the speed to high and continue to mix for an additional 5 minutes.
  2. When the dough looks smooth and is clearing the bowl, it's ready. Divide into 4 even pieces (225g each) and shape into balls.
  3. Place each ball into an oiled container and cover. Allow to sit at room temperature for 30 minutes then refrigerate for 24+ hours.
  4. Add all ingredients into the crushed tomato can and blitz with immersion blender until smooth.
  5. Mix shredded mozzarella and cheddar cheeses together.
  6. Remove shaped dough ball(s) from the fridge and bring to room temperature for about 90 minutes.
  7. To shape dough, flip tempered dough ball onto a floured work surface. Roll out into a bout a 10″/25cm round.
  8. Dock dough with a fork (poke surface of crust with a fork to create many tiny holes). Place rolled out dough into an oiled 12″ pizza pan (i’m using a 12″ lloyd’s pizza pan) and spread out smoothly into the pan, pressing perimeter of crust up the sides of the pan a bit.
  9. Add a few spoonfuls of sauce on top of the dough and spread from edge to edge.
  10. Top that with a couple of handfuls of the cheese blend and any other toppings you’d like. I like Italian sausage(recipe below), roasted red bell peppers, pepperoni, pepperoncinis, etc.
  11. Dust the top with grated parmesan and a sprinkle of flakey salt. Place in 550F/288C oven on top of a preheated pizza steel/stone to bake for 6 minutes.
  12. When cheese is melted, transfer the pizza from the pan directly onto the pizza steel. Continue to bake for 3-5 more minutes to crisp the bottom.
  13. Mix together, working the meat well.

Enchiladas Verdes

Serves 8
Ingredients
  • 1 kg boneless skinless chicken thighs
  • kosher salt
  • 375 g chicken stock or water
  • 25 g Better Than Bouillon chicken base
  • 275 g white onion cut into 1-inch chunks
  • 700 g tomatillos husked, about 20, you should have 625g after husking & halving
  • 375 g poblano peppers 3-4 peppers, destemmed and halved
  • 25 g garlic
  • 25 g cilantro leaves and stems
  • 650 g Monterey Jack cheese shredded
  • 200 g tomatillo sauce
  • 16 corn tortillas
  • 100 g sour cream mixed with 20-25g water
  • little gem or romaine lettuce shredded
  • 100 g queso fresco crumbled
  • fresh cilantro for garnish
Instructions
  1. Generously salt the chicken thighs on both sides and let them sit for 10 minutes.
  2. In a saucepan over medium-high heat, combine 375g (1½ cups) of chicken stock and the seasoned chicken thighs. Bring the mixture to a simmer. Try to make sure the chicken thighs are as submerged as possible.
  3. Cover the saucepan with a lid and reduce the heat to low. Simmer the chicken for about 25 minutes. The thighs will be done when they are shreddable, but not falling apart (For chicken breasts, cook until the internal temperature reaches 155°F/68℃ to avoid dryness.)
  4. Preheat your broiler or oven to its highest setting.
  5. Arrange, in a single layer, the tomatillos, onions, garlic, and poblanos, skin side up on a sheet tray lined with parchment paper. You may need two sheet trays depending on the size of the vegetables.
  6. Put the sheet tray on the highest tier in your oven so the contents are as close to the broiler as possible. Broil on high about 10-15 minutes until the vegetables have charred and caramelized.
  7. Transfer the charred vegetables to a blender and blend on high for about 1 minute until smooth. Add in 25g (½ cup) of cilantro to the blender and continue to blend until fully broken down.
  8. By this time, the chicken thighs should be finished cooking. Remove them from their cooking liquid and set them aside to cool slightly.
  9. Pour the pureed tomatillo sauce into the pot with the remaining cooking liquid along with 25 grams (1 ¾ tablespoons) Better Than Bouillon chicken base.
  10. Bring the mixture to a simmer and cook on medium-low for about 20 minutes, reduce the sauce by roughly 30-40% until it has thickened slightly and has developed a sweeter flavor.
  11. Remove the pan from the heat and set aside.
  12. Shred the cooked chicken into threads about ¼ inch wide and 2-3 inches long.
  13. In a bowl, combine the shredded chicken with 300 g (half) of the shredded Monterey Jack cheese and 200 g (¾ cup) of the cooked tomatillo sauce and stir until combined.
  14. Heat the tortillas in a non-stick pan over medium heat until they are pliable. Store the hot tortillas in a tortilla warmer or cover them with a dry towel.
  15. On a plate, brush each tortilla with a thin layer of tomatillo sauce on both sides.
  16. Place 65 grams (about 2 heaping tablespoons) of the chicken filling in the center of each tortilla.
  17. Roll up each tortilla tightly and place seam-side down on a parchment-lined baking sheet.
  18. Repeat with all tortillas, arranging them in pairs on the sheet.
  19. Preheat the oven to 450°F.
  20. Spread a few large spoonfuls of tomatillo sauce over the top of the enchiladas.
  21. Sprinkle about 40-50 grams (1/4 cup) shredded Monterey Jack cheese over each pair of enchiladas.
  22. Bake for 10-12 minutes until the cheese is melted and starting to brown.
  23. To serve, top with shredded little gem or romaine lettuce for crunch. Add a generous zigzag of the prepared crema (sour cream mixed with water) and sprinkle crumbled queso fresco and fresh cilantro on top.

Faster, Better, Spaghetti & Meat Sauce

Serves 0
Ingredients
  • Makes 4 portions
  • 1 lb 80/20 ground chuck 450g
  • 8 oz hot italian pork sausage 225g, I use 2 links
  • 200 g or 1 med-lrg onion medium diced
  • 20 g or 4 cloves garlic minced/pressed
  • Coarse salt
  • 1 g or 1/2tsp dried oregano
  • 1 g or 1/2tsp red chili flake
  • 75 g or 1/3c tomato paste
  • 15 g or 1Tbsp beef better than bouillon
  • 28 oz/800g can crushed tomatoes
  • 28 oz water 800g, enough water to fill tomato can
  • 7-8 g or 2tsp granulated sugar
  • 10 g or 3Tbsp fresh basil chopped
  • 12 oz dried spaghetti 340g, broken in half
  • 50 g or 1/2c grated parmesan + extra for garnish
Instructions
  1. Heat a dutch oven or large heavy bottomed pot over medium high. Add a bit of oil, the ground beef, and sausage, breaking the meat apart into small pieces as it cooks – i use a meat smasher for this.
  2. When the meat begins to brown and is well broken down, stir in the onion, garlic, and a pinch of salt, making sure to scrape up any meat fond stuck to the bottom of the pot. Cover and cook for 5 minutes.
  3. Now is a good time to preheat your oven to 425F/220C.
  4. After 5 minutes of cooking, stir in oregano, chile flake, and tomato paste and cook for a minute or so until tomato paste is well incorporated.
  5. Stir in bouillon and can of crushed tomatoes, water, 6-8g/1.5tsp salt, 7-8g/2tsp sugar and bring to a simmer.
  6. Stir in basil. Add in spaghetti and gently stir and press down to submerge noodles. Simmer for about 60 seconds.
  7. Carefully stir noodles again to fully submerge noodles. Transfer the pot to the oven to cook, uncovered for 16-18 min, gently stirring and submerging at 5 and 10 minutes into cooking. Taste for doneness and seasoning and adjust if necessary. Stir in grated parm.
  8. Garnish with additional parm.

Fettucini Alfredo with Homemade Pappardelle

Serves 4
Ingredients
  • 280 g AP flour
  • 2 large eggs
  • 4 egg yolks
  • 25-30 g water
  • 350 g pasta water
  • 150 g nice tasting unsalted butter i’m using kerrygold
  • 150 g grated parmesan good quality, be sure the only ingredient is parmesan
Instructions
  1. Create a well with the flour. Combine whites, yolks, and water and stir to combine. Dump into the flour well. Use a fork to slowly incorporate a small amount of flour in with the egg/water mixture. The idea is to not break the well…but i always do so don't worry about it if the dam breaks. Just work it by hand for about 8-15 min or until dough becomes glossy and smooth. Add a little water along the way if needed. Wrap dough, refrigerate, and allow to rest and hydrate for about 20min.
  2. Cut dough into 2 even pieces. Wrap 1 piece in a damp paper towel while you work with piece 1. Using a rolling pin, roll out the pasta dough until it is about a ½ inch thick. Using a pasta roller (I'm using a KitchenAid with an attachment) feed the dough through the pasta roller. When the dough has gone through, fold the adjust the roller to the next thinnest setting and feed the pasta dough back through. Fold the Pasta dough into itself to make a small square that can still go through the pasta roller. Open the pasta roller back up to the widest setting and feed the dough back through. When the dough is through, adjust the roller to the next thinnest setting. Repeat sending the dough through the net thinnest setting until the pasta is about as thick as two pieces of construction paper stacked on top of each other. Repeat this process until you are See video @2:40 for instructions on how to roll and cut pasta. Boil pasta for about 3.5 min or until al dente in well salted water. Toss to be sure it doesn't stick together.
  3. Next, on a surface dusted with semolina flour and cut the pasta dough into 10 inch long pieces. Dust each piece generously with more semolina.
  4. Fold each sheet of pasta into itself by thirds and set on a cutting board and cut each sheet into ¾ inch/2 cm wide strips. After all noodles are cut, unravel them and fluff them up together with more semolina.
  5. Bring a pot of heavily salted water to a boil and cook the noodles for about 3 minutes. Be sure to stir the noodles while they're cooking to make sure they don't stick together.
  6. Heat saute pan over low heat. Once pasta is cooked, transfer from water into saute pan (along with any pasta water that comes with it). Add in 250g/1 cup pasta water. Toss the pasta together and add in 150g/¾ cup of butter. Swirl to emulsified. Add in most of the 150g/1 cup of grated parmesan. Stir to combine and remove from the heat. If needed, add another ¼ cup pasta water, stir.
  7. Add the rest of the parmesan and another pad of butter. What you're doing here is trying to achieve a nice creamy, opaque sauce that's not too thin, not too thick. That will require some adjusting depending on what you're working with.
  8. To serve, top with additional grated parmesan and fresh cracked black pepper.

French Dip Sandwiches

Serves 4
Ingredients
  • 1000 g onions julienned
  • 50 g unsalted butter
  • 50 g water
  • 8 g salt
  • 900 g beef stock
  • 14 g powdered gelatine
  • 30 g Beef Better than Bouillon
  • 50 g Worcestershire
  • 8 g salt
  • 75 g mayonnaise
  • 75 g sour cream
  • 50 g prepared horseradish
  • 3 g salt
  • 2 large soft hoagie rolls 12-14 inch
  • 680 g medium roast beef thinly sliced
  • 8 slices provolone cheese
Instructions
  1. Combine the julienned onions, 8g salt, butter, and 25g (a splash) water in a 5-7 qt heavy bottom pot. Cover with a lid and cook over medium to medium high heat, stirring every 5 minutes, until onions have softened and released their moisture, about 10-15 minutes.
  2. Remove lid, reduce heat to med-low, and stir every 2 minutes until moisture evaporates and onions begin to brown and fond develops on the bottom of the pan.
  3. Add a splash of water (about 3-4 Tbsp) to deglaze, using a wooden spoon to scrape up any browning from the pan. Repeat this process every 5-7 minutes for 35-50 minutes until the onions are caramelized and softened.
  4. Combine beef stock, Worcestershire, bouillon, and 5g salt in a saucepan. Whisk in the powdered gelatin while the liquid is still cool, then bring the jus to a simmer over medium heat. Reduce by half. This should take 10 minutes.
  5. In a small bowl combine sour cream, mayonnaise, prepared horseradish, and 3g salt. Stir until completely smooth. Taste or seasoning, then keep in fridge until sandwich time.
  6. Cut off the ends of the hoagie rolls. Cut each in half so you have 4 rolls total, then slice each in half lengthwise. Put the halves back together then toast rolls in 400℉ (200℃) oven for 5 minutes.
  7. Pull rolls from the oven, open the halves, and add 2 slices of provolone over the bottom half of each roll. Return to the oven (open-faced) and toast for another 3-4 minutes until the cheese has melted.
  8. Bring the just temperature to a warm 130-140F (57°C) and swirl beef slices through the liquid for 15-20 seconds to just warm through.
  9. Transfer 4 slices of beef (170g) to the bottom of each roll, making sure to ribbon and layer each slice of beef.
  10. Add a thin layer of caramelized onions on top of the beef and spread a thin layer of horseradish sauce on each top roll. Close the sandwiches and press firmly to glue everything together. Gently cut in half with a serrated knife.
  11. Taste the hot jus one last time for seasoning. It should be borderline salty. Serve in a warm ramekin next to your sandwiches for dipping.

French Onion Soup

Serves 8
Ingredients
  • 2700 g beef stock
  • 900 g 90/10 ground beef
  • 2700 g beef stock
  • 30 g BTB beef
  • 63g powdered gelatin 21g per packet
  • 2500 g onions sliced
  • 115 g butter
  • 60 g water
  • salt
  • 1000 g water for deglazing
  • 2 g thyme
  • 5 g black pepper
  • 10 g flour
  • 150 g dry sherry
  • 25 g Worcestershire
  • 2500 g beef stock
  • 0.5 g MSG opt
  • 8 slices sourdough
  • 30 g oil/butter
  • 150 g Gruyère
  • 8 slices provolone
Instructions
  1. If making more flavorful beef stock, load beef and stock into pressure cooker, cook on high pressure 25 minutes, release pressure, strain off beef, reserve for soup
  2. Peel onions, cut pol to pole in half, then cut in half again, and julliiene into thin 1.5″ strips
  3. In large pot (at least 6 qts, preferably larger) Add in onions, stick of butter, strong pinch of salt, and large splash water, top with lid and steam onions down, stirring every 2 minutes
  4. 10 minutes later, take off lid and slowly caramelize the onions over medium to medium high heat for AT LEAST 45 minutes, but probably closer to 60. Deglaze every 5-8 minutes, keep a close eye. When deglazing add about ½ cup of water each time to make sure ALL of the brown bits are getting steamedoff and picked up. Keep the onions moving pretty frequently to avoid burning, if using electric use lower heat, those scorch faster than gas (I know from messing this up myself)
  5. Once the onions are DEEPLY caramelized and cooked down until jammy and totally soft, add in thyme and black pepper then saute with onions for 2-3 minutes, then add in sherry, deglaze, scrape up brown bits nad cook until ALLL the way reduced, we need to get rid of any and all alcoholic flavors
  6. Next add in 15g of AP flour, stir until lumpless and homogenous, cook for 3 seconds then add in stock and bring to a boil
  7. Once boiling, cook for 10 minutes more for flavors to meld, and for onions to soften even more
  8. Taste for salt, this would be the time to add in the pinch of MSG if you are comfortable using. Just like szechuan peppercorns, a little goes a very long way.
  9. To serve toast bread in butter, top with grated gruyere, and 1 slice of provolone, broil util browned and melty, then slide into hot soup which has been seasoned proper;yl with salt (Be meticulous on tasting for salt, soups are made or broken in this step)
